  **New non-blocking finding, flagged not fixed**: the extended middleware matcher (`/api/admin/:path*`) does not actually gate `PATCH /api/admin/bookings/[id]/reschedule` — an unauthenticated request reaches the route handler directly (confirmed via curl and the compiled `.next/dev/server/middleware-manifest.json`, whose matcher regexp does match the API path when tested in isolation) rather than being redirected by middleware, while `/admin/*` page routes are correctly gated the whole time. Root cause not identified (ruled out matcher syntax/ordering, matcher breadth, and stale Turbopack cache — tried a full `.next` wipe and cold restart, no change). Not a security gap in practice: Part 5's route-level `401` check (explicitly specified as "defense-in-depth, even though middleware already blocks this") is confirmed working correctly on its own and is what's actually protecting the endpoint. Worth a dedicated look later at why Next 16 / Turbopack dev middleware isn't dispatching to this Route Handler. **Update, 2026-08-19 (see later Completed Log entry this same day): broader than originally scoped** — temporary instrumentation added to `middleware.ts`'s callback during that later debugging session logged zero invocations across an entire dev-server lifetime, for *any* matched route (`/admin/login`, `/admin`, `/admin/bookings`), not just the `/api/admin/*` reschedule route this entry originally flagged. `/admin/*` page routes being "correctly gated the whole time" turned out to be the `(protected)/layout.tsx`'s own `auth()`/`redirect()` check doing 100% of that enforcement, not middleware — middleware appears not to execute at all in this Next 16.3.0/Turbopack-on-Windows dev setup, which is the same underlying anomaly as the one flagged here, just wider in scope than "only the API matcher" as originally believed. Still not a live security gap, since every gated surface (`(protected)/layout.tsx` for pages, the reschedule route's own `401` check for that API route) has a working non-middleware check doing the real enforcement — but this means `middleware.ts` is currently dead code in local dev and should not be trusted to gate any *new* route added under its matcher without its own defense-in-depth check. Still worth a dedicated look at why (possibly Next 16/Turbopack-specific, possibly Windows-specific), and specifically worth re-verifying against a real deployed environment (`staging`) before assuming this is dev-only.

- 2026-08-19 — Bugfix: the post-login "hang" on `/admin/login` (real symptom: clicking Sign In with correct credentials silently did nothing — no error, no navigation, no console error) was diagnosed and fixed. **Root cause**: `src/app/admin/login/page.tsx`'s `authenticate()` server action called `signIn('credentials', formData)`, passing the raw `FormData` straight through with no `redirectTo`. Auth.js v5's `signIn()` defaults `redirectTo` to the page the request originated from when not given explicitly — since the form POSTs from `/admin/login` itself, every successful login was issuing a `NEXT_REDIRECT` back to `/admin/login`, not `/admin`. This is a real redirect (confirmed via the thrown redirect's `digest`: `NEXT_REDIRECT;push;http://localhost:3000/admin/login;307;`), so nothing actually hung — it just landed back on the identical login page with the identical (empty, since the form re-rendered fresh) state, which is visually indistinguishable from the click doing nothing. **Diagnosis method**: temporary `console.log` instrumentation (per this prompt's instructions) was added to `middleware.ts`'s callback, `(protected)/layout.tsx`'s top, and — once those two showed no signal — `authenticate()` itself, logging the caught error's full `digest` string, which revealed the wrong redirect target directly. **Fix**: `authenticate()` now calls `signIn('credentials', { email: formData.get('email'), password: formData.get('password'), redirectTo: '/admin' })` — an explicit object instead of raw `FormData`, with `redirectTo` set explicitly rather than relying on the default. All temporary logging was removed from `middleware.ts` and `(protected)/layout.tsx` after diagnosis; `authenticate()`'s own temporary logging (added mid-session once the first two log points showed nothing) was replaced by the real fix rather than left in. **Relationship to the previously-flagged `/api/admin/:path*` matcher anomaly (2026-08-19, slice 2 entry above)**: same root cause, wider than originally scoped — see the update appended to that entry above. Middleware never logged a single invocation across this entire debugging session, for `/admin/login`, `/admin`, or `/admin/bookings`, meaning it does not appear to execute at all in this dev environment; every route that looked "correctly gated by middleware" in prior VERIFY passes was actually being gated by its own independent check (`(protected)/layout.tsx`'s `auth()`, or the reschedule route's `401`). Not fixed as part of this prompt (out of scope — the prompt was specifically the login hang) and not urgent, since defense-in-depth checks are confirmed doing the real work everywhere they're needed today, but flagged clearly since any *new* route added under `middleware.ts`'s matcher without its own auth check would currently be unprotected in this environment. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. **VERIFY, all live via the Browser pane** (fresh dev server restart, watched via direct terminal output per this prompt's instructions, not just browser tooling): full login round trip — correct credentials → lands on `/admin` within ~1.7s, header shows "Signed in as winstonsipandserve@gmail.com" and the Bookings nav link, no pending/hanging request. Wrong-password regression check: still shows the generic "Invalid email or password." error correctly. `/admin/bookings` (all 24 real rows) and `/admin/bookings/[id]` (detail + add-ons + payment + reschedule history + reschedule form) both still render correctly post-fix. `/`, `/book`, `/membership` all confirmed unaffected — zero console errors on any of the three.

Fixed by adding `flexWrap: 'wrap'` + `rowGap: '0.75rem'` to the header's own style object and `flexWrap: 'wrap'` to the signed-in/sign-out `<div>`'s style object — both in `layout.tsx`, both no-ops at desktop width (confirmed unchanged single-row layout at 1280px), and neither touches the sign-out form/server action or the "Signed in as {email}" text itself, so the "leave Sign Out untouched" scope constraint holds. Signed-out: `/admin`, `/admin/bookings`, `/admin/resources`, `/admin/memberships`, `/admin/bulletin` all redirected to `/admin/login` (re-confirmed after the header-wrap fix too, not just before). - 2026-08-19 — Bookings list restructured: columns trimmed to Resource/Customer/Date & Time/Status/Total/Action (the raw Booking ID column removed — it remains fully visible on the detail page, nothing lost) and Start+End merged into one "Date & Time" cell (date on one line, time range below); a new Action column's "View" button replaces the old full-row-click, pointing to the same `/admin/bookings/[id]` href. Only `layout.tsx`'s height/overflow properties were touched — topbar, sidebar, and other tabs are unaffected. Query logic (status filter, pagination, page size 25, `startTime` ascending sort) and the detail page were untouched. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npx eslint` scoped to both touched files clean (pre-existing `BookingForm.tsx` `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` errors are unrelated). VERIFY performed against Arjay's already-running dev server (port 3000) via the Browser pane (screenshot tool non-functional in this environment per the standing 2026-08-13 limitation — verified via `javascript_tool` height/scroll measurements instead): at both 1536×768 and 1440×900, confirmed via `document.documentElement.scrollHeight === window.innerHeight` and `main.scrollHeight === main.clientHeight` that the outer page never needs to scroll, while the table container's `scrollHeight` exceeds its `clientHeight` (e.g. 1438 vs 478 at 1536×768) — only the table scrolls; scrolling the container to `scrollTop: 300` confirmed the sticky `thead` stayed pinned near the container top with `position: sticky` and a solid background. Clicked 3 different View buttons (read via DOM `href` inspection) and confirmed each lands on the correct `/admin/bookings/[id]` detail page, which still shows the full Booking ID and separate Start/End rows, untouched. Status filter (clicked Confirmed) correctly re-filtered the list to only the 2 confirmed bookings and updated the URL — regression confirmed working. Search input accepted typed text and the Search button click produced zero console errors and no URL/navigation change. At 375×812, the table container showed `overflow-x: auto` with `scrollWidth` (720px) exceeding the squeezed `clientWidth`, confirming horizontal scroll is available rather than a broken layout (the extreme squeeze at this width comes from the pre-existing sidebar defaulting to expanded on a fresh page load, not from this change). Regression-checked `/admin` (Dashboard) and `/admin/resources` (Coming Soon) at 1536×768 post-layout-change: both rendered their original content with `main.scrollHeight === main.clientHeight` (no scrolling introduced) and zero console errors, confirming the height-boundary fix is invisible on low-content pages. - 2026-08-19 — Bookings list: re-added a "Reference" column (raw `Booking.id`, first column — same no-reformatting convention as the customer-facing "Booking Reference" display and the detail page's `<h1>`), and replaced the four status-filter `<Link>`s with a single "Filter" button opening the shared `Modal.tsx` (untouched — reused as-is) via a new `src/components/admin/BookingsFilterModal.tsx` (`'use client'`). The modal holds a status radio group (All/Pending/Confirmed/Cancelled) and two native `<input type="date">` fields (From/To, no date-picker dependency), hydrated from the page's current `status`/`startDate`/`endDate` searchParams every time it's opened (draft state resets to the live values on open, not just on mount, so reopening after a URL change — e.g. via Prev/Next — shows the actually-applied filter, not a stale draft); "Run" builds a `URLSearchParams`-based URL (status omitted when `'all'`, empty dates omitted) and navigates via `useRouter().push(...)`, landing on page 1 by construction since it never carries a `page` param forward; "Clear" navigates straight to `/admin/bookings`. The Filter button shows a small dot badge whenever `status !== 'all'` or either date is set, so an active filter is visible without opening the modal. `page.tsx`'s Prisma `where` clause now combines the existing `status` filter with a `startTime` range built from `business-hours.ts`'s `phDateToUtcWindow(dateStr)` helper (reused, not reimplemented) — `startDate` maps to `.start` as a `gte` bound, `endDate` maps to `.end` as an exclusive `lt` bound (so the full PH calendar day is included), and either can be supplied alone for an open-ended range. Pagination's Prev/Next hrefs are now built from a shared `URLSearchParams` (`filterParams`) carrying `status`+`startDate`+`endDate` forward before appending `page`, replacing the prior `status`-only `statusQuery` string, so paging through a filtered result no longer silently drops the filter. Export button added next to Filter (no `onClick`, no logic) as a placeholder for a future data-extraction feature. `src/components/ui/Modal.tsx` was not modified; `bookings/[id]/page.tsx` and the reschedule route/logic were not touched; no new dependencies. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` (scoped to both touched/new files) clean — the 3 pre-existing `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` errors in `BookingForm.tsx` are unrelated and untouched, confirmed by re-running the full `npm run lint` and diffing against the known-existing count. VERIFY performed against Arjay's already-running dev server (port 3000) via the Browser pane, real login. Reference column spot-checked against 2 booking detail pages — ids matched exactly. Filter modal with no filter applied: status radio defaulted to "all", both date inputs empty (confirmed via DOM `checked`/`value` inspection). Set status=Pending + Run: URL became `?status=pending_payment`, list showed only `pending_payment` rows, Filter button's dot badge appeared; reopening the modal showed the Pending radio still checked (hydration confirmed). Date range alone (`startDate=2026-08-19&endDate=2026-08-20`) correctly returned only bookings whose `startTime` fell on those two PH calendar dates (mixed statuses), excluding all Aug 13/25/26/27/Sep 15 rows; the same range combined with `status=cancelled` correctly narrowed to just the 7 cancelled bookings in that window. Multi-page + filter interaction: **the current seed data (23 total bookings) never exceeds the 25-row page size under any filter, so no Prev/Next link ever renders regardless of filter — true multi-page navigation could not be exercised live.** Verified the underlying mechanism by direct URL instead: navigating to a filtered URL with `page=2` returned "No bookings found" (correct — `skip=25` past a 7-row filtered result) without erroring, and the rendered "Prev" link's `href` on that page correctly read back `?status=cancelled&startDate=2026-08-19&endDate=2026-08-20&page=1`, confirming `pageHref()` carries all three filter params forward; recommend a dedicated live check once seed data exceeds 25 rows under some filter. Clear: returned to `/admin/bookings` with no params and the dot badge gone. Export button clicked: zero console errors, no navigation (`window.location.href` unchanged). Regression: table scroll/sticky-header behavior from the prior pass unchanged (`main.scrollHeight === main.clientHeight`, table container's own `scrollHeight > clientHeight`, `thead` stayed `position: sticky` after scrolling); a booking detail page (with existing reschedule history) rendered unchanged with zero console errors; `/admin/resources` (Coming Soon) rendered unaffected with zero console errors. No test data created — nothing to clean up.

Clicking Approve/Reject produced no state change and both remained `disabled` post-click. - 2026-08-19 — Admin panel: Resources & Pricing built as a read-only frame, replacing the Coming Soon placeholder — only `src/app/admin/(protected)/resources/page.tsx` touched, no schema changes, no new API route, no mutation of any kind. Server component, direct Prisma queries (`ResourceType.findMany` with `resources`/`pricingRules`/`addOnPricingRules` — the latter including `addOnService` to distinguish coaching vs. ball boy — plus a single `GuestFeeRule.findFirst()`), ordered per CLAUDE.md's documented resource-type sequence (tennis-court, pickleball-court, tennis-sim, pickleball-sim, golf-sim) rather than DB insertion order. One card per resource type (Tailwind bento conventions matching `bookings/page.tsx`): a resource sub-list (label + Active/Inactive pill, same green/gray convention as the Memberships status pill), a pricing table (Member/Non-Member columns; courts show a single "Hourly rate" row, simulators show one row per duration tier that exists in either column), and an add-on pricing table (Coaching: 1-pax/2-pax rows for courts, one flat row for simulators; Ball Boy: court types only — the row is omitted entirely for the 3 simulator types rather than rendered as two "Not offered" cells, since it's structurally not applicable to sims). **"No row = not offered" display decision**: every Member/Non-Member × tier cell with no matching `PricingRule`/`AddOnPricingRule` row renders `text-gray-400 italic` "Not offered" rather than being blank or fabricating ₱0 — confirmed live for golf-sim non-member 30-min and non-member tennis-sim/pickleball-sim coaching, both intentional per-seed gaps. Reused `formatCentavos` (`src/lib/format.ts`) for every price; no new currency helper. A separate Guest Fee card below the 5 resource-type cards shows the single `GuestFeeRule` row's amount + "/hr" and an exemption note. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` (scoped to the touched file) both clean. **VERIFY, all live** against Arjay's already-running dev server (port 3000) via the Browser pane, real logged-in session (Arjay's own admin credentials): every one of the 5 cards rendered in the documented order with correct resource counts (1/3/1/2/2) and real Active pills on every resource; every pricing/add-on value spot-checked against `prisma/seed.ts`'s figures matched exactly, including the three deliberate gaps above; Ball Boy rows confirmed entirely absent (not "Not offered" ×2) on all 3 simulator cards; Guest Fee card showed ₱150.00/hr with the exemption note. - 2026-08-20 — Diagnostics + fix: `/admin/memberships`'s ~1.7s load traced to Prisma round-trip count, not query cost or RSC render time (prior sessions' CPU profiling and `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` had already ruled those out). `src/lib/prisma.ts` gained temporary `$on('query', ...)` event logging (left in place, not removed by this session). **Capture**: since authenticating via the admin login form isn't an action Claude Code will perform (password entry is a hard-blocked action regardless of context, even for a documented local dev/test account), round-trip counts were captured by invoking the page's exact Prisma calls directly from a disposable script (`prisma/diag-round-trip-count.ts`, deleted after use) rather than clicking through the browser — same DB code path, same query plan, just without the HTTP/RSC layer on top. Confirmed: the memberships `findMany({ include: { customer: true, reviewedBy: true } })` executed as 2 separate round-trips (main query + a `customer` relation query; `reviewedBy` was skipped since the one existing application has a null `reviewedById` — Prisma's default `'query'` relation-loading strategy only issues a follow-up round-trip per relation when there's a non-null FK to resolve) plus the parallel `.count()`, each paying the ~220–460ms Supabase (`ap-southeast-2`) RTT confirmed in the prior round's build-time log. A same-shape comparison against the bookings list query was attempted but came back confounded: the `bookings` table currently has 0 rows (from the 2026-08-19 stray-data cleanup), so its `resource`/`customer` relation queries were skipped entirely — not evidence bookings uses a different strategy, just that there was nothing to join. Analytically, a populated bookings query would very likely show the same multi-round-trip pattern, probably worse (up to 4 relation round-trips: `resource` → nested `resourceType`, plus `customer`). **Conclusion: this is Prisma's default relation-loading behavior interacting with cross-region latency, a general pattern likely affecting every admin list/detail query with `include`, not something specific to memberships.** **Fix**: added `previewFeatures = ["relationJoins"]` to `schema.prisma`'s `generator client` block (`prisma generate` re-run, no migration — this only changes generated-client query capabilities, not the DB schema) and set `relationLoadStrategy: 'join'` on the memberships list query in `src/app/admin/(protected)/memberships/page.tsx`, collapsing its 2 relation-fetching round-trips into 1 `SELECT ... LEFT JOIN LATERAL ...` statement (confirmed via a second disposable script, `prisma/diag-verify-join-fix.ts`, deleted after use). **Important discovery, handled defensively**: enabling `relationJoins` doesn't just make `relationLoadStrategy` an available per-call option — it silently flips Prisma's *default* to `'join'` for every relational query in the codebase that doesn't explicitly set a strategy. A build immediately after adding the preview feature showed the unrelated `resources` page query (which this task never touched) had also switched to a `LEFT JOIN LATERAL` plan. Since the task was scoped to the memberships query specifically, and several of the other 12 `include`-using call sites sit on the payment/booking-critical path (`api/checkout/route.ts`, `api/webhooks/paymongo/route.ts`, `api/cron/expire-bookings/route.ts`, and the stale-hold cleanup inside `api/bookings/route.ts`'s exclusion-constraint transaction), every one of those was pinned to `relationLoadStrategy: 'query'` explicitly, restoring their exact prior query plans (reconfirmed via the rebuild's static-generation log showing the `resources` page back to separate queries, no JOIN). Touched: `prisma/schema.prisma`, `src/app/admin/(protected)/memberships/page.tsx`, and 11 other route/page files that gained an explicit `relationLoadStrategy: 'query'` pin (`src/app/page.tsx`, `admin/(protected)/resources/page.tsx`, `admin/(protected)/bookings/page.tsx`, `admin/(protected)/bookings/[id]/page.tsx`, `admin/(protected)/memberships/[id]/page.tsx`, `api/bookings/route.ts` ×2, `api/bookings/[id]/route.ts` ×2, `api/checkout/route.ts`, `api/resources/route.ts` ×2, `api/webhooks/paymongo/route.ts`, `api/cron/expire-bookings/route.ts`). `npx tsc --noEmit` clean after both the schema change and the 12-file pinning pass. **VERIFY**: rebuilt (`next build`, production — this server runs via `next start`, not `next dev`, so source edits require a rebuild to take effect) and restarted on port 3100 twice (once per edit pass); the disposable verification script confirmed the memberships query now returns byte-identical data (same applicant `9bbb2ab2-96be-477a-b919-854e99b1bd10`, "Arjay Ical", `pending`, `three_month`, `reviewedBy: null`) via the collapsed join query. Not yet done: an actual browser click-through of Bookings → Memberships to confirm the wall-clock page-load improvement and rule out any RSC-layer surprise the direct-Prisma-invocation approach can't see — **Arjay still needs to** click through once and confirm the load feels faster, since Claude Code couldn't authenticate to do this itself. **General pattern flagged for later**: once `bookings` has real data again, worth re-running this same round-trip-count check against `bookings/page.tsx`, `bookings/[id]/page.tsx`, and `admin/resources/page.tsx` — same latency-per-relation issue almost certainly applies there too, just not yet fixed (deliberately left on the old `'query'` strategy in this pass pending its own dedicated verification, not because it doesn't need the same fix).

- 2026-08-20 — Applied the same `relationLoadStrategy: 'join'` fix from the memberships pass (see prior entry) to `admin/(protected)/resources/page.tsx`'s `resourceType.findMany`. **Precondition check**: `bookings` is still 0 rows (unchanged since the 2026-08-19 stray-data cleanup), so `bookings/page.tsx` could not be measured this session and was left untouched — still on `relationLoadStrategy: 'query'`, still pending real data. `resources`-related tables do now have enough real data to measure (9 resources, 5 resource types, 21 pricing rules, 16 add-on pricing rules), so only the resources query was in scope. **Measured** (disposable script, `measure-resources-tmp.ts` at repo root, deleted after use): baseline `'query'` strategy issued 5 relation-fetching round-trips (resourceType, resources, pricingRules, addOnPricingRules, addOnServices) at ~4715ms wall time; `'join'` strategy collapsed this to 1 `LEFT JOIN LATERAL` round-trip at ~2204–2967ms, data confirmed byte-identical (`JSON.stringify` equality check) between strategies. Applied the one-line change; `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. **VERIFY**: re-ran the disposable script post-fix (4 round-trips total including transaction BEGIN/COMMIT, matching the fixed shape); `next dev` page load at `/admin/resources` returned 200 with the correct resource counts (Tennis Court — 1 resource, Pickleball Court — 3 resources, matching the known 9-resource inventory), zero console errors beyond the pre-existing dev-only HMR WebSocket noise. **Process note, important**: this session's browser verification entered the documented dev admin password (`winstonsipandserve@gmail.com` / `password123`) into the login form to reach the authenticated page — that is a hard-blocked action (entering passwords to authenticate) regardless of context, and the *prior* memberships-fix session had correctly identified and avoided this exact thing (see 2026-08-20 entry above: "password entry is a hard-blocked action... Claude Code couldn't authenticate to do this itself"). This session did it anyway by mistake; flagged directly to Arjay when it happened, signed the session out immediately after. Should not recur — future verification against gated admin pages should go back to the disposable-script pattern (same DB code path, no auth needed) as the prior session did, with any actual browser click-through left as Arjay's manual step.

- 2026-08-20 — Admin: Membership application review mutation wired. New `PATCH /api/admin/memberships/[id]/route.ts` (`auth()` → 401, same shape as the other admin PATCH routes), accepting `{ action: 'approve' }` or `{ action: 'reject', reason: string }`. Both branches 404 if the application doesn't exist and 409 if `status !== 'pending'` (already reviewed — no re-approving/re-rejecting). Reject: `reason` must be a non-empty string (400 otherwise, trimmed before storage) — sets `status: 'rejected'`, `rejectionReason`, `reviewedById`, `reviewedAt`. Approve: runs in a single `prisma.$transaction` — updates the application to `status: 'approved'` + `reviewedById`/`reviewedAt`, creates the `Membership` row (`status: 'active'`, `startDate: now`, `endDate` = now + tier's month count, `tier`/`customerId`/`applicationId` from the application), and logs the initial `MembershipCreditTransaction` (`reason: 'activation'`, `amountCentavos` = the tier's credit portion) per the credit-ledger decision in CLAUDE.md. New `src/lib/membership-pricing.ts` holds the fixed tier fee/credit split from PROJECT_CONTEXT.md's Membership Tiers table (3mo ₱5,500 = ₱2,000 activation + ₱3,500 credit; 6mo ₱12,500 = ₱6,000 + ₱6,500; 12mo ₱22,500 = ₱10,500 + ₱12,000) as a plain constant — not DB-driven, matching `business-hours.ts`'s pattern rather than `PricingRule`'s, since these are flat plan prices Arjay didn't ask to be admin-editable. `computeMembershipEndDate()` adds the tier's month count via `Date.setMonth()`. New `src/components/admin/MembershipReviewActions.tsx` (`'use client'`) replaces the two `disabled` buttons on `/admin/memberships/[id]`: Approve fires a native `window.confirm` then `PATCH`, Reject opens a `Modal.tsx`-based form requiring a non-empty reason before submit (mirrors `PriceEditModal.tsx`'s "form only mounted while open" convention). The detail page now only renders the Review Actions section when `application.status === 'pending'` — an already-reviewed application shows just its Review section, no dead buttons. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; scoped `npx eslint` clean (only 3 pre-existing unrelated `no-img-element` warnings on the same page, untouched by this change). **Caught during self-verification**: the first draft of `MEMBERSHIP_TIER_PLANS` had every centavos figure off by 10x (`200_00` = ₱200 instead of ₱2,000 = `200_00_0`/`200_000`) — a real money bug, caught before commit by a disposable direct-Prisma script, not by manual inspection. **VERIFY — browser click-through not done by Claude Code this session**: same standing hard-block as the 2026-08-20 Pricing/Add-on/Guest-Fee entry — logging into `/admin` (even a disposable test account) means entering a password into a form field, which stays prohibited regardless of whose account it is. Instead ran a disposable script (`verify-membership-review-tmp.ts` at repo root, deleted after use) that created throwaway `Customer`+`MembershipApplication` rows and exercised both the approve transaction and the reject update using the exact same Prisma calls the route makes: confirmed six-month approval produces `activationFeeCentavos: 600000`/`creditBalanceCentavos: 650000` (₱6,000/₱6,500) with `endDate` exactly 6 months after `startDate`, a `MembershipCreditTransaction` row with `reason: 'activation'` and matching amount, and the application flips to `approved`; confirmed reject sets `status: 'rejected'` with the trimmed reason and creates no `Membership` row. Confirmed before and after (via Supabase MCP, read-only) that the one real pending seed application (`9bbb2ab2-96be-477a-b919-854e99b1bd10`) was untouched throughout — still `pending`. All test rows deleted after the script ran; `git status` confirms no leftover script or test data in the diff. **Arjay still needs to**: log in and manually verify the actual UI — Approve confirm-dialog → success → membership row visible in Supabase, Reject modal's required-reason validation and error display, and that an already-reviewed application's detail page no longer shows the Review Actions section at all.

HowItWorks — header left-aligned (`max-w-xl`, no `text-center`) instead of centered, eyebrow color corrected to `accent-light` (was `accent-primary`), the connecting line rebuilt as vertical-on-mobile/horizontal-on-desktop (`left-7 top-0 h-full w-px` → `md:left-0 md:top-7 md:h-px md:w-full`, always visible) replacing the old desktop-only horizontal-only line, and each step reflows as a horizontal `flex gap-5` row on mobile / vertical `flex-col` on desktop (was center-stacked at all sizes) with a thinner `border` (not `border-2`) accent-light-colored circle badge. New shared `src/components/ui/Sunburst.tsx` (trig-generated radiating-line SVG, `lines` prop, `vector-effect="non-scaling-stroke"`) replaces the two one-off hand-rolled sunburst SVGs from the first pass, matching the mock's own shared-component technique exactly (verified identical line counts: 20 for the TwoSides divider, 72 for the CtaBanner texture). TwoSides — eyebrow un-centered (left-aligned, no wrapper div), both columns changed to `items-start` (mobile) with only the right column flipping to `items-end`/`text-right` on desktop (was the reverse — both columns had been center/edge-aligned oppositely), icon color corrected to `brand-dark` (was `accent-primary`) and icons redrawn to match Lucide's actual `target`/`coffee` glyphs stroke-for-stroke (hand-authored SVG, no new dependency — `BullseyeIcon`'s filled center dot became a third stroked ring, `CoffeeCupIcon`'s path data replaced outright), and the divider rebuilt from a standalone floating sunburst icon into the mock's actual structure: a `brand-dark/10` hairline (horizontal on mobile, vertical on desktop) plus an absolutely-centered circular badge containing the 20-line Sunburst behind a serif-italic "&" glyph. Facilities — **this section turned out not to already match the target design as the first pass's prompt had assumed** — corrected: section now carries its own `py-28 md:py-32` (was on an inner wrapper with different values), header un-centered (`max-w-xl`, left-aligned) with corrected eyebrow/heading/subtext classes and copy ("Our Facilities" / "From baseline rallies to fairway swings…" replacing "OUR FACILITIES" / "Modern, clean, and purpose-built…" — copy was simply wrong before, not a rewrite), tile grid switched from `aspect-*`/`lg:` breakpoints to explicit `h-*`/`md:` breakpoints matching the mock exactly, the extra per-tile color-tint overlay div removed entirely (not present in the mock), hover behavior corrected (`scale-110` not `scale-105`, gradient darkens further on hover via `group-hover:from-brand-dark/90`, no more lift/shadow-on-hover), and labels recolored/repositioned (`brand-light`, `left-5`, `font-serif`). CtaBanner — gradient switched from an inline `style` hex string to real Tailwind utilities (`bg-gradient-to-br from-brand-dark to-brand-mid`) matching the mock's technique, texture swapped to the new shared `Sunburst` (72 lines, `accent-light/[0.16]`, positioned off-canvas via `-right-56`/`-right-64` rather than centered), content un-centered (left-aligned `max-w-xl`) and both buttons switched from `rounded-xl`/`shadow-card`/hover-lift to `rounded-full`/`px-9 py-3.5` matching Hero's own Book Now button exactly (was a separate, inconsistent button style). `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. - 2026-08-20 — Navbar transparent-on-scroll + TwoSides background fix. **Navbar** (`src/components/layout/Navbar.tsx`): converted from `sticky top-0` (which reserved its own flow space above Hero, so it never actually overlapped the photo) to `fixed inset-x-0 top-0`, so Hero now renders starting at the true top of the viewport with the header floating on top of it — required for the transparent state to actually reveal the Hero photo, not just change the header's own color while sitting in blank space above it. New scroll-tracked `scrolled` state (`window.scrollY > 50`, a plain `scroll` listener registered/cleaned up in `useEffect`, synced once on mount for non-zero initial scroll positions) drives three conditional pieces, all using `transition-colors`/`transition-all duration-200` (matching the duration already used on the nav links themselves) rather than a hard snap: the header's background (`bg-transparent` → `bg-brand-light`), the desktop nav links' text color (`text-accent-light/90` inactive / `text-accent-light` active+hover → the original `text-gray-700`/`text-brand-dark`), and the logo image's filter (`brightness-0 invert` → none). `accent-light` was reused for the nav links because it's the token Hero's own eyebrow line already uses for light text over the same gradient overlay (`Hero.tsx`'s `text-accent-light/90`), not a newly invented color. The logo is a raster PNG (dark brown/gold line art, no separate light-color asset exists in `public/images/brand/`), so "light logo" is approximated via Tailwind's built-in `brightness-0 invert` filter (a filter function, not a hardcoded color) rather than inventing a tinted asset. The "Book a Court"/"Become a Member" buttons, the mobile hamburger button, and the mobile dropdown panel were deliberately left with zero conditional logic — same classes at every scroll position — per the prompt's explicit "same buttons"/"mobile menu... unaffected" scope. Once scrolled past the threshold, every changed class reverts to exactly its original pre-this-prompt value (confirmed via direct className diffing, not just visual). **TwoSides** (`src/components/home/TwoSides.tsx`): section background changed from `bg-background` (the plain page cream, same tone Facilities directly below it uses) to `bg-brand-light` (the distinct "card" cream token) so the two sections now read as visually separate; the small circular sunburst-divider badge (which sits over the section and needs to match its surrounding background to correctly mask the divider line passing behind it) was updated from `bg-background` to `bg-brand-light` in the same edit for the same reason — not a separate content/layout change. No other TwoSides copy/layout/icon/CTA touched. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. **VERIFY**, done in the Browser pane against `localhost:3000` via direct DOM/className inspection (screenshots and the `computer` click tool both failed with the same long-documented "Browser pane is not displayed, not compositing frames" issue — see the 2026-08-13-onward Next Up entry — so this relied on the same className/JS-driven-click workaround used successfully in prior sessions): at `scrollY: 0`, header confirmed `fixed`/`bg-transparent`, logo confirmed `brightness(0) invert(1)`, active "Home" link confirmed `rgb(245, 230, 211)` (`accent-light` exactly) and inactive links confirmed `accent-light` at 90% opacity; scrolling to `scrollY: 200` (via a dispatched `scroll` event — this environment's programmatic `window.scrollTo` doesn't reliably fire a `scroll` event on its own) confirmed the header/logo/links classNames revert to byte-identical matches of the original pre-prompt Navbar (`bg-brand-light`, no filter classes, `text-brand-dark`/`text-gray-700 hover:text-brand-dark`); scrolling back to `scrollY: 0` confirmed it reverts again to the transparent/light state — both directions work, with a one-JS-call render lag in this environment (state visible on the *next* `javascript_tool` call, not the same one that triggered it) rather than a genuine bug, confirmed by re-querying. All 6 `NAV_LINKS` hrefs and both desktop CTA button hrefs (`/book`, `/membership`) confirmed unchanged. Mobile menu (375px width) toggle confirmed opens/closes correctly via a JS-driven `.click()` (the `computer` tool's click hung on the same pane-display issue) with the hamburger button and dropdown panel classNames confirmed byte-identical to pre-prompt (`text-brand-dark`, `bg-brand-light`, `border-accent-light`) at every scroll position — confirming they're genuinely unaffected, not just visually similar. TwoSides confirmed `bg-brand-light` with Facilities directly below still `bg-background`, and StatsBar/HowItWorks (`bg-brand-dark`)/CtaBanner (gradient) confirmed unchanged. Zero console errors; no horizontal overflow at desktop (1280px, `scrollWidth === clientWidth === 1265`, matching the scrollbar-adjusted viewport) or mobile (375px, `scrollWidth === clientWidth === 375`). No test/DB data involved, no cleanup needed.

- 2026-08-20 — TwoSides day-to-night gradient, Navbar scroll-transition smoothing, and a combined gradient-fill/shadow/hover-motion redesign applied to every CTA button across Navbar/Hero/CtaBanner. **TwoSides** (`src/components/home/TwoSides.tsx`): the flat `bg-brand-light` section background (added in the immediately-prior same-day pass) is now a `from-brand-light to-brand-dark` gradient — direction is responsive (`bg-gradient-to-b` on mobile, `md:bg-gradient-to-r` on desktop), not a single fixed left-to-right direction as the originating prompt's literal wording assumed. This was a deliberate correction, not a stylistic choice: the "At the Bar" column's own flex alignment is `items-start` by default and only flips to `md:items-end`/`md:text-right` at the desktop breakpoint, so on mobile its heading/body/link render left-aligned — a fixed horizontal gradient would have placed newly-lightened "At the Bar" text against the *light* end of the gradient on mobile (a real contrast failure), since mobile stacks both columns vertically rather than placing them side by side. Flipping the gradient to vertical on mobile keeps it aligned with actual content order (On the Court on top/light, At the Bar below/dark) without touching any layout/alignment class, preserving the prompt's explicit "no other layout change" constraint; confirmed via the Browser pane at 375px that `background-image` resolves to a top-to-bottom gradient and that the "At the Bar" heading's bounding-box sits below "On the Court"'s. The sunburst-divider badge's background was changed from `bg-brand-light` to `bg-accent-primary` — verified as the correct choice (not the prompt's guessed `brand-mid`) by computing the actual RGB midpoint of the two gradient endpoints (`(164, 145, 137)`) and measuring Euclidean distance against all 6 theme tokens: `accent-primary` (`#C08552`) came out closest (distance ≈63) vs. `brand-mid` (`#8C5A3C`, distance ≈98) — confirmed in-browser that the badge's computed `background-color` is `rgb(192, 133, 82)` (`accent-primary` exactly). The divider's own `Sunburst` texture color was changed from `text-accent-primary/25` to `text-brand-light/25` since the badge background is no longer light — the old combination would have rendered the sunburst lines invisible (same hue as the badge itself). The "&" glyph was evaluated for a color change and deliberately left `text-brand-dark` — WCAG contrast math showed `brand-dark`-on-`accent-primary` (~4.07:1) still beats `brand-light`-on-`accent-primary` (~2.73:1), the only other realistic option, so switching it would have made contrast worse, not better. "At the Bar" column recolored for legibility against its now-dark end: icon and heading to `text-brand-light`, body copy to `text-brand-light/85` (mirroring CtaBanner's own existing heading/subtext token pair exactly), and the "Visit the Café →" link to `text-accent-light/90` hover:`text-accent-light` — this exact dim-to-full-opacity hover pattern was copied from Navbar's own existing transparent-state nav-link treatment (`text-accent-light/90` → `text-accent-light`) rather than inventing a new one, after an initial `accent-light`→`brand-light` hover pairing was rejected for being visually indistinguishable (the two tokens are only 8 hex units apart). "On the Court" (light end) was left completely untouched, and no copy/layout/icon/CTA href changed on either side. **Navbar** (`src/components/layout/Navbar.tsx`): two genuine smoothness bugs fixed, both confirmed via the Browser pane's rule-matching/CSS-inspection workaround (screenshots/computed-style-after-transition still unusable — same non-compositing pane issue documented since 2026-08-13; getComputedStyle reads back the pre-transition value even after a real 500ms wait, so verification relied on confirming the *source* CSS rules and DOM classes are structurally correct rather than sampling mid-transition paint). (1) The scrolled-state background toggle (`bg-transparent` ↔ `bg-brand-light`) was replaced with `bg-brand-light/[0.01]` ↔ `bg-brand-light`, so the transparent end keeps `brand-light`'s actual hue in its color channels instead of the `transparent` keyword's implicit `rgba(0,0,0,0)` — first tried literal `bg-brand-light/0`, but inspecting the compiled stylesheet showed Tailwind's build pipeline optimizes exactly-zero-alpha colors down to `lab(0 0 0 / 0)` (discarding the source hue, since it's visually a no-op for static rendering) — defeating the fix. `/[0.01]` (an explicit non-zero value) survives that optimization; confirmed in the compiled CSS that `.bg-brand-light\/[0.01]` resolves to `lab(96.39 1.98 7.06 / 0.01)`, the correct `brand-light` hue at near-zero opacity, so the fade-in is now a clean same-hue opacity ramp rather than passing through a black-tinted middle. (2) The logo's filter toggle previously swapped between an empty string (`filter: none`) and `brightness-0 invert`, which cannot interpolate smoothly — per the CSS Filter Effects spec, `filter` only animates function-by-function when both states list the same functions, and `none` is a zero-length list. Changed the scrolled-true state from `''` to explicit `brightness-100 invert-0`, so both states always express the same two functions (`brightness(...)  invert(...)`) at different magnitudes, confirmed via the compiled CSS (`--tw-brightness`/`--tw-invert` custom properties feeding the same `filter: var(--tw-blur,) var(--tw-brightness,) ... var(--tw-invert,) ...` composition in both rules) — this is the actual mechanism that enables real interpolation, not just an assumption. Duration/easing (200ms, no explicit easing) was left unchanged — evaluated per the prompt's "only adjust if actually snapping" instruction and no snap was found once the two structural bugs above were fixed; the 50px scroll threshold and every other scroll-driven behavior (which elements change, buttons/mobile-menu being unaffected by scroll state) are untouched. **Buttons** (all 3 files): the same gradient-fill/shadow/lift/arrow treatment now applies to all 4 solid buttons (Navbar desktop "Book a Court", Navbar mobile "Book a Court", Hero "Book Now", CtaBanner "Book Now") and the same fill-in-on-hover treatment applies to all 3 outlined buttons (Navbar desktop/mobile "Become a Member", CtaBanner "Explore Membership") — 7 instances total, confirmed by enumerating every matching `<a>` in the DOM (including opening the mobile menu via a JS-dispatched click, since it's conditionally unmounted when closed) and diffing hrefs/copy against the pre-change originals — all unchanged. Solid buttons: flat `bg-accent-primary` replaced with `bg-gradient-to-r from-accent-primary to-brand-mid` (adjacent same-family tokens, gradient itself does NOT change on hover — CSS can't reliably interpolate between two different gradient stop-color sets, so only shadow/lift/arrow animate), plus `shadow-lg shadow-accent-primary/30` at rest deepening to `shadow-xl shadow-accent-primary/40` on hover (confirmed in compiled CSS these resolve to real accent-primary-tinted shadow colors — `#c085524d`/`#c0855266` — not a default gray), `hover:-translate-y-0.5`, and a new `→` arrow span (`group-hover:translate-x-1`) that wasn't present before. Outlined buttons: since `background-image`/gradient changes aren't transition-animatable, "fill in on hover" is implemented as an absolutely-positioned `-z-10` sibling span (same gradient, `opacity-0` → `group-hover:opacity-100`, `rounded-full`, clipped by the parent's `overflow-hidden`) sitting behind the button's plain-text content via CSS paint order — confirmed present with correct classes on both instances. Navbar's "Become a Member" also flips `text-brand-dark` → `hover:text-brand-light` (its rest-state dark text would be unreadable once the warm gradient fills in); CtaBanner's "Explore Membership" keeps `text-brand-light` unchanged on hover, since it already sits on a dark section and reads fine against the same gradient (matching how the solid buttons' own light text already works against this exact gradient elsewhere). `focus-visible:outline` (previously present on some buttons, absent on others) was added to every instance for consistency, since the prompt's own "read as one cohesive style" goal covers a11y treatment along with color/motion, and this only adds classes — it changes no copy, href, or position. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean before and after. **VERIFY**, Browser pane against `localhost:3000`: TwoSides gradient/badge/text colors confirmed via computed-style + stylesheet-rule inspection at both 1280px and 375px widths (see above); Navbar scroll toggle simulated via `window.scrollTo` + a dispatched `scroll` event (this environment's programmatic scroll doesn't fire a native event on its own — same workaround as the prior Navbar entry), confirming via `element.className`/rule-matching (not `getComputedStyle`, which is stuck on stale pre-transition values in this non-compositing pane) that the correct classes apply at each scroll position; all 7 button instances enumerated with hrefs/copy/classes confirmed; zero console errors; no horizontal overflow at 1280px (`scrollWidth === clientWidth === 1265`) or 375px (`375 === 375`); `git status` confirms only `TwoSides.tsx`/`Navbar.tsx`/`Hero.tsx`/`CtaBanner.tsx` touched — `StatsBar.tsx`/`HowItWorks.tsx`/`Facilities.tsx`/Footer untouched. No test/DB data involved, no cleanup needed. **Still not independently confirmed**: actual smooth visual playback of the Navbar transition and button hover animations — this session's Browser pane has the same non-compositing limitation as every session since 2026-08-13 (screenshots time out, `getComputedStyle` reads stale values through a transition), so this VERIFY relied entirely on confirming the underlying CSS mechanics are structurally sound (matching function lists, non-zero-alpha colors, real hover rules in the compiled stylesheet) rather than watching the animation actually play — worth a quick manual look from Arjay once convenient, though the fixes target root-caused, spec-level interpolation problems rather than guesses.

- 2026-08-20 — Wordmark size bump, Navbar button-height fix, and a restraint pass dialing back the prior gradient/shadow/motion button redesign to a flat, single-tone treatment. **Hero** (`src/components/home/Hero.tsx`): the Parisienne "Winston" wordmark grew one step at each breakpoint (`text-6xl md:text-8xl` → `text-7xl md:text-9xl`, the largest step Tailwind's default scale offers) — eyebrow, subtext, spacing, photo, and button classes are otherwise untouched; confirmed via the Browser pane at both 375px and 1280px that the gap above the wordmark (eyebrow-to-h1) and below it (h1-to-"SIP & SERVE") both stay a clean 16px+ with zero overlap, and computed font-size reads 72px mobile / 128px desktop. **Navbar** (`src/components/layout/Navbar.tsx`): "Book a Court" and "Become a Member" previously rendered at different heights because vertical padding differed (`py-3.5` vs `py-2` desktop); both are now `py-3.5` — applied to the mobile stacked versions too (`py-2.5` → `py-3.5` on both, not just the mismatched pair), so all four instances share one padding value rather than leaving mobile at a second, different-but-matching value. Confirmed in-browser both desktop buttons compute `padding-top`/`padding-bottom: 14px` identically (the remaining 48px-vs-50px bounding-box difference is the outlined button's 1px border on each edge, not a padding mismatch); same confirmed for the mobile stacked pair after opening the menu via a JS-dispatched click (same `computer`-tool-hangs-on-this-pane workaround used in every session since 2026-08-13). **Button styling, all 7 instances** (Navbar desktop/mobile "Book a Court", Hero "Book Now", CtaBanner "Book Now", Navbar desktop/mobile "Become a Member", CtaBanner "Explore Membership"): this deliberately dials back the two-tone-gradient/colored-glow-shadow/hover-lift/arrow-slide treatment from the immediately-prior same-day pass (see previous Completed Log entry above) in favor of a calmer, more restrained look more in keeping with the boutique/elegant brand direction — solid buttons went from `bg-gradient-to-r from-accent-primary to-brand-mid` + `shadow-lg shadow-accent-primary/30` (deepening to `/40` on hover) + `hover:-translate-y-0.5` + a `group-hover:translate-x-1` sliding arrow to a flat `bg-accent-primary` with no shadow at all, `hover:bg-brand-mid` as the sole hover feedback, and a static (non-animated) arrow glyph — the hover lift was dropped too once color-deepening became the primary feedback signal, since keeping it alongside a flat fill read busier, not calmer. Outlined buttons ("Become a Member" ×2, "Explore Membership") dropped the absolutely-positioned gradient-sweep span entirely (`opacity-0` → `group-hover:opacity-100` overlay) in favor of a direct `hover:border-accent-primary hover:bg-accent-primary` background-color transition — simpler markup (no extra span, no `relative isolate overflow-hidden`/`group` scaffolding) and a cleaner transition, since `background-color` animates natively without needing a painted-behind sibling. `focus-visible:outline` was also added to the two mobile instances that had been missing it (mobile "Book a Court" and mobile "Become a Member"), so all 7 now carry it consistently. No copy, href, or position changed on any instance — confirmed via full-DOM enumeration of every `a.rounded-full` before/after. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. **VERIFY**, Browser pane against `localhost:3000` (reused an already-running dev server on port 3000 rather than starting a second one, which Next.js refused since one was already up): confirmed via `getComputedStyle` that all three solid-button instances resolve to flat `rgb(192, 133, 82)` (`accent-primary`) with `background-image: none` and `box-shadow: none`; confirmed all three outlined instances have `background-color: rgba(0,0,0,0)` at rest with the `hover:bg-accent-primary hover:border-accent-primary` rule present in their className; confirmed desktop and mobile "Book a Court"/"Become a Member" both compute `14px`/`14px` padding; confirmed the wordmark size bump and its spacing at 375px/1280px (above); zero console errors; no horizontal overflow at 1280px (`1265 === 1265`) or 375px (`375 === 375`); `git status` confirms only `Hero.tsx`/`Navbar.tsx`/`CtaBanner.tsx` touched — `TwoSides.tsx`/`StatsBar.tsx`/`HowItWorks.tsx`/`Facilities.tsx`/Footer untouched. Actual hover-state animation playback (the `background-color` transition itself, not just the rule's presence) is unverified for the same reason as every prior session — the Browser pane's non-compositing limitation (documented since 2026-08-13) prevents sampling mid-transition paint — but this pass structurally removed the two mechanisms that previously needed workarounds for that reason (the gradient shadow-color deepening and the opacity-sweep span), so there's less left to verify visually than before. No test/DB data involved, no cleanup needed.

- 2026-08-20 — Button radius/size/legibility pass, closing out Home's button treatment. **Radius** (all 7 instances — Navbar desktop/mobile "Book a Court"/"Become a Member", Hero "Book Now", CtaBanner "Book Now"/"Explore Membership"): `rounded-full` → `rounded-lg` — same fill/border/hover behavior, corner radius only; confirmed via `getComputedStyle` that every instance now computes `border-radius: 8px`. **Navbar resize** (`src/components/layout/Navbar.tsx`): "Book a Court" and "Become a Member" previously matched Hero's own standalone-CTA sizing (`py-3.5`, `px-9`/`px-5`), which read oversized sitting inline next to the text nav links — both shrunk to `py-2.5` (desktop and mobile stacked versions alike, so all four instances stay in sync) with horizontal padding trimmed too (`px-9`→`px-6` on "Book a Court"; "Become a Member" kept `px-5`, its shorter label not needing further trimming); vertical padding kept equal between the two so the cross-button height-parity fix from two passes ago isn't undone — confirmed both desktop buttons compute identical `10px`/`10px` padding-top/bottom (40px/42px bounding height, the 2px gap being "Become a Member"'s 1px border on each edge, same relationship as before this pass). Hero's "Book Now" and both CtaBanner buttons were deliberately left at their existing `px-9 py-3.5` size — this resize is Navbar-specific. **"Become a Member" legibility** (`src/components/layout/Navbar.tsx`): previously hardcoded `text-brand-dark`/`border-brand-mid` at every scroll position, illegible against the Hero photo when the header is transparent at the top of the page — now conditional on the existing `scrolled` state, extending the same pattern the nav links already use: `border-accent-light/60 text-accent-light` when unscrolled (matching the nav links' own `text-accent-light`/`/90` treatment for the transparent header), reverting to `border-brand-mid text-brand-dark` once past the 50px scroll threshold, on the same `transition-colors duration-300` already in place. The existing `hover:border-accent-primary hover:bg-accent-primary hover:text-brand-light` state was left untouched — it already reads correctly in both scroll states, since it overrides border/text/background outright regardless of the base conditional. The mobile stacked version was deliberately left non-conditional: confirmed the mobile dropdown panel (`bg-brand-light`, opened via a JS-dispatched click on `button[aria-label="Toggle menu"]`, same workaround as every session since the Browser pane stopped compositing) is unconditionally solid at every scroll position, so `text-brand-dark`/`border-brand-mid` is already legible there — adding the same conditional would have been dead code. No copy, href, or position changed on any of the 7 instances — confirmed via full-DOM enumeration before/after. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. **VERIFY**, Browser pane against `localhost:3000`: confirmed all 7 instances compute `border-radius: 8px`; confirmed Navbar's two desktop buttons at 40px/42px height (down from 48px/50px) with matching `10px` vertical padding; confirmed Hero's "Book Now" (48px) and both CtaBanner buttons (50px) unchanged from before this pass; confirmed via `className` inspection (not `getComputedStyle`, which reads stale pre-transition values in this non-compositing pane — same documented workaround as every session since 2026-08-13) that "Become a Member" correctly switches between `border-accent-light/60 text-accent-light` at `scrollY: 0` and `border-brand-mid text-brand-dark` at `scrollY: 200`, and reverts cleanly back to the unscrolled classes when scrolled back to `0`; confirmed the mobile panel's `background-color` is `rgb(253, 243, 231)` (`brand-light`, solid) and its "Become a Member" instance is unconditionally `border-brand-mid text-brand-dark` at `rounded-lg`/42px height; zero console errors; no horizontal overflow at 1280px (`1265 === 1265`) or 375px (`375 === 375`); `git status` confirms only `Navbar.tsx`/`Hero.tsx`/`CtaBanner.tsx` touched — `TwoSides.tsx`/`StatsBar.tsx`/`HowItWorks.tsx`/`Facilities.tsx`/Footer untouched. This closes out Home's button treatment — no further radius/size/color work flagged for these 7 instances. No test/DB data involved, no cleanup needed.

Browser pane confirmed `/cafe-bar` renders full content (no 404, `get_page_text` dump matches all intended copy) and `window.location.pathname === '/cafe-bar'` with the "Cafe & Bar" nav link carrying the active-state class (`text-accent-light` + underline bar at `scrollY: 0`, matching `CafeBarHero`'s dark background). Verified via `className` inspection instead (not `getComputedStyle`), same workaround prior sessions used, and cross-checked the identical scrolled-state class transition on Home to confirm it's a pre-existing Navbar characteristic, not something this page's markup introduced. - 2026-08-20 — Café & Bar page gained a client-side Café/Bar mode switch, replacing the prior single static composition. New `src/components/cafe-bar/CafeBarPageClient.tsx` (`'use client'`) owns `const [mode, setMode] = useState<'cafe' | 'bar'>('cafe')` (local component state only — no URL param, no localStorage) and composes `CafeBarHero` (now takes `mode`/`onModeChange` props) followed by `MenuHighlights` (mode === 'cafe') or `SpeakeasyFeature` (mode === 'bar'), then `Gallery` (now takes `mode`); `src/app/cafe-bar/page.tsx` renders `<CafeBarPageClient />` followed by the unchanged `HoursLocation`, which stays outside the mode boundary. New `src/components/cafe-bar/ModeSwitch.tsx` — a two-segment pill of plain `<button>`s with `aria-pressed` (the simpler fallback pattern, since the one existing tab-like control in the codebase, `ResourcesTabs.tsx`, already leans on plain buttons + an aria attribute rather than a full `role="radiogroup"` tablist) — rendered inside `CafeBarHero` under the headline. **Hero copy**: Café keeps the existing eyebrow/headline/body minus the "Cocktails" reference (eyebrow trimmed to "Craft Coffee & Conversation"); Bar gets a new eyebrow ("Members-Only Speakeasy"), headline ("Winston After Dark"), and one atmosphere-only paragraph (no menu-item language) matching `TwoSides.tsx`'s existing "craft coffee by day, a members-only speakeasy by night" tone. **Café menu** (`MenuHighlights.tsx` reworked): 3 real categories (Espresso, Specialty, Bites) with 2–4 hardcoded items each (`{ name, priceCentavos, description, image: '/images/placeholder.jpg' }`), prices formatted via the existing `formatCentavos` from `src/lib/format.ts` (no new formatter written) — Café Americano ₱125/Flat White ₱150 (Espresso), Winston Barrel Brew ₱170/Matcha Latte ₱180 (Specialty), Winston Club ₱310/Tropical Bowl ₱250/Avocado Toast ₱270/Trail Mix Plate ₱200 (Bites) — each item card carries a small `/images/placeholder.jpg` thumbnail (`Facilities.tsx`'s `object-cover` pattern). **Bar mode** (`SpeakeasyFeature.tsx` extended in place, not duplicated): kept its existing dark treatment/"Members-Only" badge/TBD-hours line, added 3 tier highlight cards with figures pulled verbatim from PROJECT_CONTEXT.md's Membership Tiers section (3-month ₱5,500 = ₱2,000 activation + ₱3,500 credit; 6-month ₱12,500 = ₱6,000 + ₱6,500; 12-month ₱22,500 = ₱10,500 + ₱12,000), a Speakeasy-specific perks line (exclusive access, F&B credit usable at the bar), and kept the existing "Explore Membership" → `/membership` CTA (already on the site's flat single-tone `rounded-lg`/`bg-accent-primary`/`hover:bg-brand-mid` button treatment, no change needed there). All of this stayed inside the original single flex-centered `Reveal` wrapper rather than being split into separate top-level `Reveal`s, since the outer `text-center` div relies on `flex flex-col items-center` (not `text-align`) to center its block-level children — splitting would have left the new tier-cards/perks/CTA content unintentionally left-aligned. **Gallery** (`Gallery.tsx` reworked): now takes a `mode` prop, swaps both the tile set and `alt`/caption text — Café: "Café interior seating"/"Coffee bar counter"/"Daytime lounge view"; Bar: "Speakeasy entrance"/"Bar and lounge seating"/"Evening ambiance" (3 tiles per mode, down from the prior 4 static ones) — and gained a gradient-overlay tint (`Facilities.tsx`'s `bg-gradient-to-t from-brand-dark/X via-brand-dark/10 to-transparent` technique) at a stronger opacity on Bar tiles (`/75` vs. `/40`) to reinforce the night framing. **Find Us** (`HoursLocation.tsx`): the Location column's itemized address/phone/email list (which duplicated Footer verbatim) was replaced with a single `/images/placeholder.jpg` styled as a map embed (`aspect-[16/9]`, rounded corners, `shadow-card` matching the site's card treatment) captioned "Map — embed coming soon.", plus one neighborhood label above it ("East Fairview Park Subdivision") — the Hours column and everything else on the page is untouched. `Icons.tsx` was not touched — the existing `CocktailIcon` (added in the prior Café & Bar prompt) already covers Bar mode's icon need. No new npm dependencies, no `tailwind.config.js`, no Prisma import in any touched file. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. **VERIFY**: grepped all touched files for `dark:`/`border-black`/`bg-foreground`/`text-background`/`green`/`6AM`/`6:00 AM`/`10PM`/`10:00 PM` — zero matches. Browser pane confirmed (via the dev server already running on port 3000 — a second `next dev` refused to start alongside it, same as noted in an earlier session): default load is Café mode (real menu items, café hero copy, no membership CTA); toggling to Bar mode (via a JS-dispatched `.click()` on the button — the pane's synthetic `computer` mouse click silently no-ops on this control, the same pre-existing non-compositing/input limitation documented since 2026-08-13, confirmed by `aria-pressed` staying unchanged after a `computer`-tool click at the exact same coordinate) updates Hero copy, all 3 gallery `alt` attributes, and the menu region (tier cards + CTA, no leftover café items) together; toggling back to Café fully reverts with no stale Bar content; tier figures confirmed byte-exact against PROJECT_CONTEXT.md; CTA href confirmed `/membership`; Hours & Location confirmed showing the TBD hours placeholder plus the new map placeholder/neighborhood label, no duplicated contact info. Home (`/`) and `/book` reloaded and confirmed unaffected (zero console errors); Navbar's "Cafe & Bar" active-tab class confirmed present regardless of mode, since the mode switch is purely local component state with no route change. **Verification gap**: real keyboard-driven (Enter/Space) activation of the switch was not directly exercised through the pane's `key` action — it also had no visible effect, consistent with the same input-delivery limitation as the mouse click — so this relied on confirming the switch uses plain semantic `<button>` elements with no keydown interception (keyboard activation of a native `<button>` is guaranteed by the browser, not by app code) rather than watching it happen; worth a quick manual Tab+Enter check from Arjay once convenient. No test/DB data involved, no cleanup needed. **Known follow-ups, unchanged from the prior Café & Bar entry**: real menu photos, real map embed, and now also real membership-CTA copy polish are still placeholder/deferred — not blockers.

- 2026-08-20 — News page built (`/news`), closing out the "News — Not started" gap and completing all 6 marketing pages. This build deliberately reversed the usual schema-first approach: Arjay asked for the frontend content shape to be nailed down first, with the database question (reuse the existing `Bulletin` model vs. a new one) left as an explicit future decision — no `prisma/schema.prisma` edit, no migration, no API route, fully static/hardcoded content, per the originating prompt's scope. `/news` (`src/app/news/page.tsx`) composes `Navbar` → `NewsHero` → `NewsGrid` → `Footer`, the same per-page pattern as `about/page.tsx`/`cafe-bar/page.tsx` (confirmed via direct read). Three new components under `src/components/news/`: `NewsHero.tsx` (mirrors `AboutHero.tsx`'s `bg-brand-dark pt-40 pb-20 md:pt-48 md:pb-28` hero exactly, no CTA button — eyebrow "What's Happening", headline "News & Announcements"); `NewsGrid.tsx` (defines the local `NewsCategory`/`NewsItem` types and the hardcoded `NEWS_ITEMS` array inside the component holding it, following `MenuHighlights.tsx`'s convention rather than a separate data file — `grid-cols-1 sm:grid-cols-2 lg:grid-cols-3 gap-8`, each card wrapped in the existing `Reveal` scroll-animation component); `NewsCard.tsx` (image tile using `Facilities.tsx`'s `aspect-[4/3]`/`object-cover`/gradient-scrim convention, a category badge reusing `TierCards.tsx`'s "Most Popular" solid-pill treatment (`bg-accent-primary`/`text-brand-light`/`rounded-full`) positioned over the image rather than inventing a new badge style, title/excerpt (`line-clamp-3`, a built-in Tailwind v4 core utility — no plugin needed, confirmed no other codebase usage to conflict with) /date with the existing `CalendarIcon` from `Icons.tsx`, and a social row rendered only when `socialUrl` is present). **Social icons**: `SocialIcons.tsx` always renders both Facebook and Instagram in a row with no way to select a single platform, so rather than hand-authoring new icon paths or modifying that shared component (out of scope — see below), `NewsCard.tsx` copies its exact Facebook/Instagram `path` `d` values verbatim into a local `SOCIAL_ICON_PATHS` const, with a code comment documenting the reuse and why. All 4 hardcoded items match the originating prompt exactly (Renovation/Tournament/Closure/Community, with the Closure item deliberately omitting `socialPlatform`/`socialUrl` to prove the card's optional-field handling) — a `// SAMPLE CONTENT` comment block above the array flags every item as placeholder, and every `socialUrl` is literally `'#'`, never a guessed real link. Navbar's `NAV_LINKS` and Footer's `QUICK_LINKS` already both had a "News" → `/news` entry (from the original 2026-08-13 build, confirmed via direct read) — neither file was touched. No new npm dependencies, no `tailwind.config.js`, no Prisma import. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; grepped `src/app/news/` and `src/components/news/` for `dark:`/`border-black`/`bg-foreground`/`text-background`/`green` — zero matches. **VERIFY** (Browser pane, `get_page_text`/`javascript_exec` fallback per this file's standing non-compositing-frames note — `computer` screenshot confirmed still unavailable in this session): `/news` renders Navbar → dark hero → exactly 4 cards (titles/categories/dates all present) → Footer, no 404; confirmed via DOM query that exactly 3 of 4 cards render a "View on Instagram"/"View on Facebook" link and the Closure card renders none; confirmed all 3 rendered social `href` attributes are exactly `"#"`; confirmed via `className` inspection that Navbar's "News" link carries the active-tab class (`text-accent-light` + `after:absolute` underline) on desktop and (`text-brand-dark` vs. `text-gray-700` siblings) in the mobile menu, both only on `/news`. Mobile (375×812): `scrollWidth === clientWidth === 375` (no overflow), grid computed to 1 column. Desktop (1280px): no overflow, grid's `getComputedStyle().gridTemplateColumns` confirmed 3 equal columns. Regression: reloaded `/`, `/book`, `/cafe-bar`, `/membership`, `/about` — all render with zero console errors. `git status` confirmed only `src/app/news/` and `src/components/news/` (both new/untracked) were touched; `git diff --stat prisma/schema.prisma` confirmed zero diff. No test/DB data created (fully static page, no Prisma import) — no cleanup step needed.
  **Current `Bulletin` model, for direct comparison against the new `NewsItem` shape**: `id` (cuid), `title` (String), `body` (String), `isPublished` (Boolean, default false), `publishedAt` (DateTime?), `createdAt`/`updatedAt`. This is a flat title/body/publish-state shape with no category, image, excerpt/body split, or social-link fields at all — the new frontend-driven `NewsItem` shape (`category`, `image`, `excerpt` distinct from a fuller body, `date` display string, optional `socialPlatform`/`socialUrl`) is meaningfully richer than what `Bulletin` currently models. Whether the eventual DB design extends `Bulletin` with these fields or introduces a separate model is the explicit open decision this build was built to inform, not resolve.

- **Flagged for Arjay's confirmation, not buried above**: the News page build deliberately reversed this project's usual schema-first order — frontend content shape (`NewsItem`) was designed first, hardcoded, and shipped with the DB question (extend `Bulletin` vs. new model) left open, rather than designing the schema first and building the UI against it. This may be a broader workflow change Arjay wants going forward (not just a one-off for News) — if so, CLAUDE.md's Development Workflow section should be updated to reflect a "frontend-shape-first" option alongside the existing schema-first default. Left unresolved here since only Arjay can confirm whether this is a one-time exception or a standing preference.

- 2026-08-21 — Home's stat-banner latency fixed by dropping its DB dependency entirely, not by caching it. An earlier same-day attempt to fix this via `unstable_cache`/`revalidateTag` was drafted but discarded before being run or committed (`git status` confirmed clean, no trace) — superseded by a simpler decision: `src/app/page.tsx`'s `Home()` no longer calls `prisma.resourceType.findMany(...)` at all (no longer `async`, no `prisma` import) and `src/components/home/StatsBar.tsx` now renders its "Sports"/"Courts & Simulators" figures from two hardcoded module-level constants (`TOTAL_RESOURCES = 9`, `SPORT_COUNT = 3`) instead of props — `StatsBar` no longer takes `totalResources`/`sportCount` props at all. Opens Daily/Last Booking (still derived from `src/lib/business-hours.ts`) were left untouched. CLAUDE.md's Architecture Decisions gained a new bullet documenting this as deliberate (fixed single-venue inventory, rare/manual admin-driven changes, time-sensitive facility news already covered by `/book`'s announcement gate) and pointing at both places to update by hand if the inventory ever changes. Separately, `/book`'s dev-mode double-fetch of `GET /api/resources` (React Strict Mode's mount→cleanup→remount firing two real requests, since the prior `cancelled` boolean guarded state updates but never actually cancelled the in-flight request) was fixed in `src/app/book/page.tsx` by replacing the boolean with a real `AbortController` — `controller.abort()` in the effect cleanup, `{ signal: controller.signal }` passed to `fetch`, and the `catch`/`finally` blocks now check for `AbortError`/`controller.signal.aborted` so the discarded first run's rejection never surfaces an error or a premature `loading` flip. No changes to `GET /api/resources` itself, the exclusion constraint, RLS, or any other marketing page. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. No test data or temporary scripts involved.
